prompt
Task

You are the acceptance reviewer. You did not plan or write this edit. Check the edit against the approved plan, the block's contract and the correctness rules.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019

chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The file tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v is the edited design. The edit is the diff below, taken against the original source.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • The edit does what the approved plan says; any change outside the regions it names is explained by the plan.
  • Every block input port is connected, and the block's outputs drive what the plan says they drive.
  • The correctness rules hold for the edited source.

The synthesis checks and the reference simulation have passed; the remaining checks run after your review.

SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (each lane is its own sum: intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8] in module activation; no block is chained)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: for lane i = 0..15 one mult_add_int_18x19: ax = {10'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, ay = {11'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, chainin = 64'b0, scanin = 19'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, clk = activation's clk, reset = act_blk_reset (derived, below); result[7:0] drives intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8], result[63:8] unused, chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.

PLAN:

What is being replaced

In activation (module at line 5417), lines 5482 and 5485 inside the for loop:

slope_applied_data_internal[i]     <= data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i];   // 5482
data_intercept_flopped[i]          <= data_intercept[i];                              // 5483
data_intercept_delayed[i]          <= data_intercept_flopped[i];                      // 5484
intercept_applied_data_internal[i] <= slope_applied_data_internal[i] + data_intercept_delayed[i]; // 5485

All arithmetic is 8-bit (DWIDTH=8): the product at 5482 is self-determined to 8 bits by its 8-bit LHS, and the add at 5485 is 8-bit. So the value to produce is

intercept_applied_data_internal[i] = (data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] + data_intercept_flopped_at_the_previous_edge[i]) mod 256

which is exactly result[7:0] of ax*ay + bx with the operands above, because the block's unsigned zero-extended 64-bit sum agrees with the original 8-bit sum modulo 256. Signedness of the intercept constants (lines 5570-5573, e.g. -8'd18) is therefore irrelevant: only the low 8 bits are read.

Register stages (per converted region)

Before the rewrite, from the module's own flops to the result:

  • data_slope -> data_slope_flopped (1) -> slope_applied_data_internal (2) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (3) = 3 stages
  • inp_data -> inp_data_flopped (1) -> slope_applied_data_internal (2) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (3) = 3 stages
  • data_intercept -> data_intercept_flopped (1) -> data_intercept_delayed (2) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (3) = 3 stages

After the rewrite:

  • data_slope -> data_slope_flopped (1, kept soft) -> block ax register (2) -> block result register (3) = 3 stages
  • inp_data -> inp_data_flopped (1, kept soft) -> block ay register (2) -> block result register (3) = 3 stages
  • data_intercept -> data_intercept_flopped (1, kept soft) -> block bx register (2) -> block result register (3) = 3 stages

Counted from the block's operands (data_slope_flopped, inp_data_flopped, data_intercept_flopped) it is 2 stages before and 2 stages after. This is T2, not T1: the design register that held the product (slope_applied_data_internal) and the second intercept register (data_intercept_delayed) are removed and the block's operand registers take their place, with the multiply and the add moved between the block's two register stages. The values at every cycle are unchanged.

Edits for the editor (all inside module activation; no other module changes)
  1. Delete the declarations of slope_applied_data_internal (5433), intercept_applied_data_internal (5434) and data_intercept_delayed (5444), and every assignment to them: lines 5467, 5468, 5470 (first clear branch), 5482, 5484, 5485 (the pipeline), 5522, 5523, 5525 (the else clear branch). Keep data_intercept_flopped and its three assignments (5471, 5483, 5526) exactly as they are — it is now the bx source. Keep relu_applied_data_internal, inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, cycle_count, activation_in_progress, done_activation_internal, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, address, data_slope, data_intercept untouched.
  2. Add wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] intercept_applied_data_internal; (now a wire, same name, so line 5534 assign out_data_internal = (activation_type) ? intercept_applied_data_internal : relu_applied_data_internal; is unchanged).
  3. Add the derived block reset — one wire shared by all 16 blocks:
wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation
                   | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)
                   | ~activation_type;
  1. Instantiate the 16 blocks with a generate/genvar loop (use a fresh genvar name, e.g. gi; i at line 5436 is a reg [31:0] used by the procedural loops and must not be reused), with a per-lane wire [63:0] for result and assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*8 +: 8] = blk_result[gi][7:0];. Connect every input port by name as given in MAPPING; leave chainout and scanout unconnected (no chaining is used, so chainin is tied to 64'b0 on every block).
Why that reset is correct

The always block at 5465-5532 gives the four removed/kept registers exactly three behaviours, and never a plain hold outside one case:

  • clear when reset | ~enable_activation (5466) or when ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) (the else at 5521): slope_applied, intercept_applied, data_intercept_delayed, data_intercept_flopped all go to 0;
  • load when active and activation_type==1'b1 (5481);
  • hold when active and activation_type==1'b0 (the ReLU branch at 5486 assigns only relu_applied_data_internal).

act_blk_reset is the complement of load, so the block loads exactly on the design's load edges and clears on both the clear edges and the ReLU edges. On a clear edge the block zeroes ax, ay, bx and result, so the product of its operand registers is 0*0 = 0 and the next sum is 0+0 = 0 — which is precisely what the original produces when slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed were cleared to 0. The two interesting boundaries:

  • ReLU-only pass (activation_type 0 for the whole pass): the original clears the four registers at the last inactive edge and then holds 0; the block holds 0 as well. Exact.
  • activation_type 0 -> 1 inside a pass: the original loads slope_applied(held)=0 + data_intercept_delayed(held)=0; the block's operand registers were cleared the cycle before, so result loads 0 too. Exact.

intercept_applied_data_internal is read only at line 5534 under activation_type, and only leaves the module through out_data when enable_activation (5461); slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed had no other reader in the file, so nothing else loses visibility. inp_data_flopped stays soft because line 5461 reads it when ~enable_activation. data_slope_flopped has no reader other than 5482 but stays soft because it supplies ax.

ASSUMES: activation_type does not go 1 -> 0 -> 1 within one activation pass, i.e. the mode bit at REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR (8'h3A, written at line 4913, reset value 1 at line 4838) is programmed before a pass and held while enable_activation is high and in_data_available | activation_in_progress stays high (lines 5477, 5509, 5517). That is the one case where the original's ReLU-branch hold (lines 5480-5489) carries a non-zero slope_applied_data_internal/data_intercept_delayed across the ReLU window and then publishes it at line 5534, which a block that clears instead of holding cannot reproduce. The testbench does not honour the assumption, but it does not reach the case either: it would need two APB writes with PADDR==8'h3A, PSEL&PENABLE&PWRITE and opposite PWDATA[0] inside one ~18-cycle activation pass (cycle_count limit DESIGN_SIZE+2, line 5504), and its random drive sets PSEL, PENABLE, PWRITE and all 8 PADDR bits independently each cycle. Every other activation_type trajectory — constant 1, constant 0, a single 1 -> 0, a single 0 -> 1 — is reproduced exactly, as shown above.

Candidates left, and why
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (256 instances): correctness/capacity. The mapping itself would be clean (ax = a, ay = b, bx = 0, chainin = 0, reset = 1'b0, result[15:0] replacing mul_out_temp_reg: a_flopped/b_flopped plus mul_out_temp_reg are exactly 2 unreset register stages, lines 4683-4693, matching the block, and the accumulator out_temp at 4699-4704 stays soft). But qmult is one module instantiated once per seq_mac and seq_mac once per PE, so converting the region converts all 256 instances, and the device has only 132 DSP tiles with one block per tile. The region cannot be placed, and it cannot be partially converted without duplicating the PE into two variants, which is outside the scope of a rewrite onto the block. Left for correctness/capacity, not "this pass".
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377(sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5070$380..$413 (all 16 lanes of line 5070): correctness. At line 5070 variance_applied_data[i] <= mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var sits under the same if (validity_mask[i]) as line 5069, so at one edge the product uses mean_applied_data as registered at the previous edge but inv_var and validity_mask[i] as sampled at this edge. The block registers ax, ay and bx together at a single edge, so no assignment of its ports reproduces operands sampled one cycle apart: it would need the next-cycle value of inv_var and validity_mask_a_rows, which only exists by duplicating cfg's APB write decode (lines 4879-4913) and routing PADDR/PWRITE/PSEL/PENABLE/PWDATA into norm. Also, validity_mask[i]==0 makes line 5074 a pass-through rather than a product, which has to be folded into ay as a 1, again needing the next-cycle mask. Not attempted this pass; an assumption that inv_var and validity_mask_a_rows are static during a normalization pass is exactly the kind the reference stimulus can break with a single random APB write, and it would corrupt every lane at once.

The rewrite adds 16 blocks (16 of 132 DSP tiles), chains none of them, and leaves 240 tiles free.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two changes would each unlock more of the design. (1) In activation, give the ReLU branch (line 5486) explicit assignments for slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal — clearing them, or letting the tanH pipeline run unconditionally and selecting only at line 5534 — so the tanH datapath has no activation_type-dependent hold; the 16 blocks above would then be unconditionally correct with reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) and no ASSUMES. (2) In norm, sample inv_var and validity_mask into module-local registers and use those for both line 5069 and line 5070 (or move the * inv_var under its own stage) so both multiply operands come from the same clock edge; that would let all 16 products of line 5070 map onto the block as well.

+// The tanH datapath (slope*input + intercept) on the mult_add_int_18x19 hard block, one block per lane.
+// The block's operand registers replace slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed, and its
+// result register replaces intercept_applied_data_internal. The block's synchronous reset is the
+// complement of the design's load condition for those registers, so the block loads exactly on the

ACCEPT

Checked against the plan, the contract and the correctness rules:

Plan conformance — The edit touches only module activation (lines 5430–5553); the subtree check in sim_run.log:3 confirms nothing outside the design's modules changed, and the diff contains no hunk outside that module. Every numbered edit in the plan is present: slope_applied_data_internal / data_intercept_delayed declarations and all their assignments removed (both clear branches and the load branch), intercept_applied_data_internal redeclared as a wire at tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5433 so tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5523 is untouched, data_intercept_flopped and its three assignments kept (5466, 5477, 5515), act_blk_reset added at 5530, and the 16-lane generate loop at 5534–5553 with a fresh genvar gi (the module's reg [31:0] i at 5435 is intact).

Ports and tie-offs — All eight input ports are connected by name: clk, reset(act_blk_reset), mode_sigs(12'b0), ax({10'b0,...}) = 18 b, ay({11'b0,...}) = 19 b, bx({28'b0,...}) = 36 b, chainin(64'b0), scanin(19'b0). chainout and scanout are left unconnected, so nothing reads chainout through general routing. 16 blocks for one activation instance (tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5946) — within the 132 DSP tiles.

Behaviour — Pipeline depth matches: block ax/ay/bx registers at edge k hold data_slope_flopped/inp_data_flopped/data_intercept_flopped from edge k−1, and result at edge k+1 is their product plus addend — identical to the original's slope_applied_data_internal + data_intercept_delayed feeding intercept_applied_data_internal. Both operands are 8-bit unsigned regs zero-extended, and only result[7:0] is read, so the block's mod-2^64 sum agrees with the original's self-determined 8-bit arithmetic mod 256 (the negative intercept literals are stored unsigned, so this is unaffected).

Reset — act_blk_reset is the exact complement of the original load condition ~reset & enable_activation & (in_data_available | activation_in_progress) & activation_type, so the block loads on exactly the design's load edges and clears on both clear branches; a cleared ax/ay/bx gives 0*0+0 = 0 into result, matching the original's zeroed slope_applied/data_intercept_delayed/intercept_applied. The only divergence is the ReLU-branch hold, which is the case the plan's ASSUMES line covers and which is invisible unless activation_type returns to 1 with stale non-zero state.

Visibility — slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed no longer appear anywhere in the file (only in the comment at 5526); the sole surviving reader of the block's output is tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5523, under activation_type. inp_data_flopped and data_slope_flopped stay as soft registers, preserving the ~enable_activation bypass at 5459/5461.

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule
57

prompt

2 paragraph breaks restored by the export, where the recorder joined text blocks with no separator.

Task

You are the planner. Read the design source, tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v, and decide which of the candidate arithmetic regions listed below to rewrite onto the hard block described next, or that none should be rewritten. Write a plan for the editor, who carries it out; do not edit any file.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design
Candidate regions

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Goal

Propose a correct rewrite onto the block. The loop measures it against the original design on area × delay; you are not asked to predict the result.

Output
Tiers
  • T1: The block replaces the region's arithmetic as it stands: the same operations on the same operands, now inside the block.
  • T2: The region's arithmetic is regrouped to fit the block (products regrouped into the block's sums, registers moved), computing the same values.
  • NONE: You propose no rewrite this time. The plan states why, from what you were given: no correct rewrite, or nothing left to try. The run then ends and keeps the best result so far if its median over every seed is below the original's, and the original design otherwise (also when no rewrite has beaten it).
Header

Write exactly this header, each key at the start of its own line, then the plan:

TIER: <exactly one of T1, T2, NONE>
REGIONS: <the converted candidate ids, comma-separated; NONE when TIER is NONE>
SHARED_REDUCTION: <the signal or module the converted products are summed in; NONE for a single region>
BLOCKS: <how many blocks the rewrite instantiates, one integer>
MAPPING: <which design signals go to which block ports>
PLAN: <the plan, stating everything listed below>

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output rules
  • Write nothing before the TIER line; read the source first.
  • Your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
  • If a change to the design itself, not to the rewrite, would let more of it use the block, you may say so on a line starting DESIGN_CHANGE:. It is reported to the user and does not change your decision.
Measured result of your plan

Each row gives the candidate's figures at seed 1, which every measured candidate was measured at, and then, where more seeds were run, the median over the seeds named, which is the figure the loop compared. The period is register to register (VPR's intra-domain critical path, the worst over its clocks clk, clk_mem). The product is the run's objective, area × delay; the ratios are against the baseline's figures (the median of five seeds, 1 to 5; A/A0 area, D/D0 period, and their product). The original design is the result to beat: the run holds it until a rewrite's area × delay is below the original's, and at the end keeps a rewrite only if its median over every seed is still below. What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which your plan rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530
SHARED_REDUCTION: NONE (each lane is its own sum: intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8] in module activation; no block is chained)
BLOCKS: 16
MAPPING: for lane i = 0..15 one mult_add_int_18x19: ax = {10'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, ay = {11'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, chainin = 64'b0, scanin = 19'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, clk = activation's clk, reset = act_blk_reset (derived, below); result[7:0] drives intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8], result[63:8] unused, chainout unconnected, scanout unconnected.

PLAN:

What is being replaced

In activation (module at line 5417), lines 5482 and 5485 inside the for loop:

slope_applied_data_internal[i]     <= data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i];   // 5482
data_intercept_flopped[i]          <= data_intercept[i];                              // 5483
data_intercept_delayed[i]          <= data_intercept_flopped[i];                      // 5484
intercept_applied_data_internal[i] <= slope_applied_data_internal[i] + data_intercept_delayed[i]; // 5485

All arithmetic is 8-bit (DWIDTH=8): the product at 5482 is self-determined to 8 bits by its 8-bit LHS, and the add at 5485 is 8-bit. So the value to produce is

intercept_applied_data_internal[i] = (data_slope_flopped[i] * inp_data_flopped[i] + data_intercept_flopped_at_the_previous_edge[i]) mod 256

which is exactly result[7:0] of ax*ay + bx with the operands above, because the block's unsigned zero-extended 64-bit sum agrees with the original 8-bit sum modulo 256. Signedness of the intercept constants (lines 5570-5573, e.g. -8'd18) is therefore irrelevant: only the low 8 bits are read.

Register stages (per converted region)

Before the rewrite, from the module's own flops to the result:

  • data_slope -> data_slope_flopped (1) -> slope_applied_data_internal (2) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (3) = 3 stages
  • inp_data -> inp_data_flopped (1) -> slope_applied_data_internal (2) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (3) = 3 stages
  • data_intercept -> data_intercept_flopped (1) -> data_intercept_delayed (2) -> intercept_applied_data_internal (3) = 3 stages

After the rewrite:

  • data_slope -> data_slope_flopped (1, kept soft) -> block ax register (2) -> block result register (3) = 3 stages
  • inp_data -> inp_data_flopped (1, kept soft) -> block ay register (2) -> block result register (3) = 3 stages
  • data_intercept -> data_intercept_flopped (1, kept soft) -> block bx register (2) -> block result register (3) = 3 stages

Counted from the block's operands (data_slope_flopped, inp_data_flopped, data_intercept_flopped) it is 2 stages before and 2 stages after. This is T2, not T1: the design register that held the product (slope_applied_data_internal) and the second intercept register (data_intercept_delayed) are removed and the block's operand registers take their place, with the multiply and the add moved between the block's two register stages. The values at every cycle are unchanged.

Edits for the editor (all inside module activation; no other module changes)
  1. Delete the declarations of slope_applied_data_internal (5433), intercept_applied_data_internal (5434) and data_intercept_delayed (5444), and every assignment to them: lines 5467, 5468, 5470 (first clear branch), 5482, 5484, 5485 (the pipeline), 5522, 5523, 5525 (the else clear branch). Keep data_intercept_flopped and its three assignments (5471, 5483, 5526) exactly as they are — it is now the bx source. Keep relu_applied_data_internal, inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, cycle_count, activation_in_progress, done_activation_internal, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, address, data_slope, data_intercept untouched.
  2. Add wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] intercept_applied_data_internal; (now a wire, same name, so line 5534 assign out_data_internal = (activation_type) ? intercept_applied_data_internal : relu_applied_data_internal; is unchanged).
  3. Add the derived block reset — one wire shared by all 16 blocks:
wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation
                   | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)
                   | ~activation_type;
  1. Instantiate the 16 blocks with a generate/genvar loop (use a fresh genvar name, e.g. gi; i at line 5436 is a reg [31:0] used by the procedural loops and must not be reused), with a per-lane wire [63:0] for result and assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*8 +: 8] = blk_result[gi][7:0];. Connect every input port by name as given in MAPPING; leave chainout and scanout unconnected (no chaining is used, so chainin is tied to 64'b0 on every block).
Why that reset is correct

The always block at 5465-5532 gives the four removed/kept registers exactly three behaviours, and never a plain hold outside one case:

  • clear when reset | ~enable_activation (5466) or when ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) (the else at 5521): slope_applied, intercept_applied, data_intercept_delayed, data_intercept_flopped all go to 0;
  • load when active and activation_type==1'b1 (5481);
  • hold when active and activation_type==1'b0 (the ReLU branch at 5486 assigns only relu_applied_data_internal).

act_blk_reset is the complement of load, so the block loads exactly on the design's load edges and clears on both the clear edges and the ReLU edges. On a clear edge the block zeroes ax, ay, bx and result, so the product of its operand registers is 0*0 = 0 and the next sum is 0+0 = 0 — which is precisely what the original produces when slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed were cleared to 0. The two interesting boundaries:

  • ReLU-only pass (activation_type 0 for the whole pass): the original clears the four registers at the last inactive edge and then holds 0; the block holds 0 as well. Exact.
  • activation_type 0 -> 1 inside a pass: the original loads slope_applied(held)=0 + data_intercept_delayed(held)=0; the block's operand registers were cleared the cycle before, so result loads 0 too. Exact.

intercept_applied_data_internal is read only at line 5534 under activation_type, and only leaves the module through out_data when enable_activation (5461); slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed had no other reader in the file, so nothing else loses visibility. inp_data_flopped stays soft because line 5461 reads it when ~enable_activation. data_slope_flopped has no reader other than 5482 but stays soft because it supplies ax.

ASSUMES: activation_type does not go 1 -> 0 -> 1 within one activation pass, i.e. the mode bit at REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR (8'h3A, written at line 4913, reset value 1 at line 4838) is programmed before a pass and held while enable_activation is high and in_data_available | activation_in_progress stays high (lines 5477, 5509, 5517). That is the one case where the original's ReLU-branch hold (lines 5480-5489) carries a non-zero slope_applied_data_internal/data_intercept_delayed across the ReLU window and then publishes it at line 5534, which a block that clears instead of holding cannot reproduce. The testbench does not honour the assumption, but it does not reach the case either: it would need two APB writes with PADDR==8'h3A, PSEL&PENABLE&PWRITE and opposite PWDATA[0] inside one ~18-cycle activation pass (cycle_count limit DESIGN_SIZE+2, line 5504), and its random drive sets PSEL, PENABLE, PWRITE and all 8 PADDR bits independently each cycle. Every other activation_type trajectory — constant 1, constant 0, a single 1 -> 0, a single 0 -> 1 — is reproduced exactly, as shown above.

Candidates left, and why
  • qmult.$mul$...:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac) (256 instances): correctness/capacity. The mapping itself would be clean (ax = a, ay = b, bx = 0, chainin = 0, reset = 1'b0, result[15:0] replacing mul_out_temp_reg: a_flopped/b_flopped plus mul_out_temp_reg are exactly 2 unreset register stages, lines 4683-4693, matching the block, and the accumulator out_temp at 4699-4704 stays soft). But qmult is one module instantiated once per seq_mac and seq_mac once per PE, so converting the region converts all 256 instances, and the device has only 132 DSP tiles with one block per tile. The region cannot be placed, and it cannot be partially converted without duplicating the PE into two variants, which is outside the scope of a rewrite onto the block. Left for correctness/capacity, not "this pass".
  • norm.$mul$...:5070$368/$371/$374/$377(sum_in=top) and norm.$mul$...:5070$380..$413 (all 16 lanes of line 5070): correctness. At line 5070 variance_applied_data[i] <= mean_applied_data[i] * inv_var sits under the same if (validity_mask[i]) as line 5069, so at one edge the product uses mean_applied_data as registered at the previous edge but inv_var and validity_mask[i] as sampled at this edge. The block registers ax, ay and bx together at a single edge, so no assignment of its ports reproduces operands sampled one cycle apart: it would need the next-cycle value of inv_var and validity_mask_a_rows, which only exists by duplicating cfg's APB write decode (lines 4879-4913) and routing PADDR/PWRITE/PSEL/PENABLE/PWDATA into norm. Also, validity_mask[i]==0 makes line 5074 a pass-through rather than a product, which has to be folded into ay as a 1, again needing the next-cycle mask. Not attempted this pass; an assumption that inv_var and validity_mask_a_rows are static during a normalization pass is exactly the kind the reference stimulus can break with a single random APB write, and it would corrupt every lane at once.

The rewrite adds 16 blocks (16 of 132 DSP tiles), chains none of them, and leaves 240 tiles free.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two changes would each unlock more of the design. (1) In activation, give the ReLU branch (line 5486) explicit assignments for slope_applied_data_internal, data_intercept_flopped, data_intercept_delayed and intercept_applied_data_internal — clearing them, or letting the tanH pipeline run unconditionally and selecting only at line 5534 — so the tanH datapath has no activation_type-dependent hold; the 16 blocks above would then be unconditionally correct with reset = reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress) and no ASSUMES. (2) In norm, sample inv_var and validity_mask into module-local registers and use those for both line 5069 and line 5070 (or move the * inv_var under its own stage) so both multiply operands come from the same clock edge; that would let all 16 products of line 5070 map onto the block as well.

MAPPING:

  • activation, lane i = 0..15 (16 blocks, unchanged from the kept candidate): ax = {10'b0, data_slope_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, ay = {11'b0, inp_data_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, bx = {28'b0, data_intercept_flopped[i*8 +: 8]}, chainin = 64'b0, scanin = 19'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, clk = activation's clk, reset = act_blk_reset (derived below); result[7:0] drives intercept_applied_data_internal[i*8 +: 8]; result[63:8], chainout, scanout unused.
  • norm, lane i = 0..15 (16 new blocks): ax = {10'b0, (validity_mask[i] ? (inp_data[i*8 +: 8] - mean) : inp_data[i*8 +: 8])} (8-bit, wrapping), ay = {11'b0, (validity_mask[i] ? inv_var : 8'd1)}, bx = 36'b0, chainin = 64'b0, scanin = 19'b0, mode_sigs = 12'b0, clk = norm's clk, reset = norm_blk_reset (derived below); result[7:0] drives variance_applied_data[i*8 +: 8]; result[63:8], chainout, scanout unused.

PLAN:

What changed from the kept candidate

The kept candidate (16 activation blocks) is retained verbatim and 16 blocks are added in norm. Reason: the measured critical path after that candidate is still a soft-register → 8x8 multiply → soft-register path (lo07069.Q → $mul~2021[0].a[5] → out[4] → lo07052.D, 0.060 + 1.940 + 2.140 + 1.662), i.e. exactly the shape of the path the activation conversion removed, with 3.6 ns of it routing to and from a shared DSP. Only two groups of multiplies can still produce that shape: norm line 5070 (16 instances) and qmult inside the PEs (256 instances). The PE group cannot be mapped at all (below), so norm is the only one left to try, and it is mappable. The blocks' registered 2.219 ns internal path replaces the long FF→DSP→FF path with two short ones (soft logic → ax setup, and result clock-to-Q → pool's adders).

Part A — activation, 16 blocks (identical to the kept candidate)

Replaces lines 5482 and 5485 in the loop at 5480-5489. All arithmetic there is 8 bit (DWIDTH=8): the product at 5482 is self-determined to its 8-bit LHS and the add at 5485 is 8 bit, so the value to produce is (data_slope_flopped[i]*inp_data_flopped[i] + data_intercept_flopped[i]@prev_edge) mod 256, which is result[7:0] of ax*ay + bx with the operands in MAPPING, because the block's unsigned 64-bit sum agrees with the original 8-bit sum modulo 256. The signed intercept constants (5570-5573) do not matter: only the low 8 bits are read.

Editor steps (module activation, lines 5417-5626):

  1. Delete the declarations of slope_applied_data_internal (5433), intercept_applied_data_internal (5434) and data_intercept_delayed (5444), and every assignment to them: 5467, 5468, 5470, 5482, 5484, 5485, 5522, 5523, 5525. Keep data_intercept_flopped and its three assignments (5471, 5483, 5526) — it is the bx source. Leave relu_applied_data_internal, inp_data_flopped, data_slope_flopped, cycle_count, activation_in_progress, done_activation_internal, out_data_available_internal, in_data_available_flopped, address, data_slope, data_intercept untouched.
  2. Add wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] intercept_applied_data_internal; (same name, so line 5534 is unchanged).

  3. Add the shared derived reset:

    wire act_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_activation
                  | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)
                  | ~activation_type;
  4. Instantiate 16 blocks in a generate/genvar loop (fresh genvar, e.g. gi; i at 5436 is a reg [31:0] used by the procedural loops and must not be reused), with a per-lane wire [63:0] blk_result; and assign intercept_applied_data_internal[gi*DWIDTH +:DWIDTH] = blk_result[DWIDTH-1:0];. Connect every input port by name as in MAPPING; leave chainout and scanout` unconnected.

Why that reset is correct: the always block at 5465-5532 gives those registers only three behaviours — clear when reset | ~enable_activation (5466) or on the else at 5521; load when active and activation_type==1'b1 (5481); hold when active and activation_type==1'b0 (the ReLU branch at 5486 assigns only relu_applied_data_internal). act_blk_reset is the complement of the load condition, so the block loads exactly on the design's load edges. On a clear edge it zeroes ax, ay, bx and result, so the next sum is 0*0+0 = 0, which is what the original produced from its cleared registers. Boundaries: a ReLU-only pass — original clears then holds 0, block holds 0, exact; activation_type 0→1 inside a pass — original loads held 0 + held 0 = 0, block's operand registers were cleared the cycle before so result loads 0, exact.

Visibility: intercept_applied_data_internal is read only at 5534 under activation_type and leaves the module only through out_data when enable_activation (5461); slope_applied_data_internal and data_intercept_delayed have no other reader in the file, so nothing loses visibility. inp_data_flopped stays soft (read at 5461 when ~enable_activation); data_slope_flopped stays soft because it feeds ax.

Part B — norm, 16 new blocks

Original, per lane i, in the always block at 5049-5105: the only two registers involved are mean_applied_data[i] (M) and variance_applied_data[i] (V), and every edge either loads them (5059: !reset && enable_norm && (in_data_available || norm_in_progress), lines 5067-5076) or clears both to 0 (5050-5052 and 5097-5099). There is no hold case.

  • load edge k: M(k) = mask(k) ? inp_data(k)-mean(k) : inp_data(k), V(k) = mask(k) ? M(k-1)*inv_var(k) : M(k-1) — all 8 bit, unsigned (inv_var is reg [7:0], 4764).
  • The block: ax carries exactly the next-M expression, so ax_reg after edge k equals M(k); ay carries mask ? inv_var : 1; result after edge k is (ax_reg*ay_reg) mod 2^64 from the values loaded at edge k-1, whose low 8 bits are M(k-1)*inv_var mod 256 (mask) or M(k-1) (no mask) — the original V(k), provided mask/inv_var did not change between edge k-1 and edge k (see ASSUMES). When edge k-1 was a clear edge, ax_reg = ay_reg = 0, so result = 0, which is exactly what the original produces then (M(k-1)=0, so V(k)=0 in both branches) — no assumption needed on that boundary, nor on mean, which ax samples at the same edge the original does.

Editor steps (module norm, lines 5007-5109):

  1. Delete the declarations at 5023 and 5024 and add wire [DESIGN_SIZE*DWIDTH-1:0] variance_applied_data; (same name, so line 5107 is unchanged). mean_applied_data disappears entirely — it is read only at 5070 and 5074, nowhere else in the file, so it may live in the block's ax register.
  2. In the always block: delete mean_applied_data <= 0; and variance_applied_data <= 0; from both clear branches (5051-5052 and 5098-5099), and delete the whole for loop at 5067-5076. Keep everything else (cycle_count, out_data_available_internal, done_norm_internal, norm_in_progress, in_data_available_flopped, inp_data_flopped) exactly as it is. Leave the reg [31:0] i; declaration at 5048 in place (now unused, harmless).
  3. Add the derived reset and the 16 blocks (fresh genvar, e.g. gi; the 8-bit operand wires keep the original's 8-bit wrapping, and the concatenations zero-extend to the port widths):
    ```
    wire norm_blk_reset = reset | ~enable_norm | ~(in_data_available | norm_in_progress);

genvar gi;
generate
for (gi = 0; gi < `DESIGN_SIZE; gi = gi + 1) begin : norm_mul
wire [DWIDTH-1:0] blk_ax = validity_mask[gi] ? (inp_data[gi*DWIDTH +: `DWIDTH] - mean)
: inp_data[gi*DWIDTH +: DWIDTH];
wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] blk_ay = validity_mask[gi] ? inv_var : 8'd1;
wire [63:0] blk_result;
mult_add_int_18x19 u_norm_mul (
.clk(clk), .reset(norm_blk_reset), .mode_sigs(12'b0),
.ax({10'b0, blk_ax}), .ay({11'b0, blk_ay}), .bx(36'b0),
.chainin(64'b0), .scanin(19'b0),
.result(blk_result), .chainout(), .scanout()
);
assign variance_applied_data[gi*DWIDTH +: DWIDTH] = blk_result[`DWIDTH-1:0];
end
endgenerate


`norm_blk_reset` is the exact complement of the load condition at 5050/5059, so the block clears on every edge the design cleared M and V and loads on every edge it loaded them. Visibility: `variance_applied_data` leaves through `out_data_internal` (5107) / `out_data` (5032) to `pool` (5915) and is still a readable wire; the `>> 1` and `>> 2` averaging in `pool` (5386, 5392) stays in soft logic, i.e. the 4-product sum of lanes 0-3 is kept outside the blocks, with every term unchanged.

## Register stages, per converted region, before and after

- `activation` lanes (16 regions): before — `data_slope`→`data_slope_flopped`(1)→`slope_applied_data_internal`(2)→`intercept_applied_data_internal`(3); same 3 for `inp_data`→`inp_data_flopped`→… and for `data_intercept`→`data_intercept_flopped`→`data_intercept_delayed`→…  After — `data_slope_flopped`/`inp_data_flopped`/`data_intercept_flopped` kept soft (1), block operand register (2), block `result` register (3). 3 stages before, 3 after; counted from the block's operands, 2 before and 2 after. T2 because the product register (`slope_applied_data_internal`) and the second intercept register (`data_intercept_delayed`) are replaced by the block's operand registers, with the multiply and the add between the block's two register stages.
- `norm` lanes (16 regions): before — `inp_data`→`mean_applied_data`(1)→`variance_applied_data`(2) = 2 stages. After — `inp_data`→ block `ax`/`ay` register (1) → block `result` register (2) = 2 stages. T2 because M becomes the block's `ax` register, V becomes the block's `result` register, and the `validity_mask` select of line 5074 is folded into `ay` as the constant 1.

## Blocks added

32 blocks, 32 DSP tiles (16 `activation` + 16 `norm`), none chained, every `chainin` tied to 64'b0. At seed 1 the kept candidate packed 104 tiles with 88 holding the design's remaining soft multiplies; removing `norm`'s 16 multiplies and adding 16 blocks leaves at most 88 + 32 = 120 of the device's 132 DSP tiles, so it fits even if no tile is freed.

## Candidates left, and why

- `qmult.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:4728$349(sum_in=seq_mac)` (256 instances) — **correctness/capacity, not "this pass".** The mapping itself would be clean (`ax = a_flopped`, `ay = b_flopped`, `bx = 0`, `chainin = 0`, `reset = 1'b0`: `a_flopped`/`b_flopped` (4683-4686) plus `mul_out_temp_reg` (4691-4693) are exactly 2 unreset register stages, matching the block, with the accumulator `out_temp` (4699-4704) left soft). But `qmult` sits in `seq_mac`, one per PE, and the array has 256 PEs, so converting the region converts all 256 instances; the device has 132 DSP tiles and a block of this mode takes a tile alone. It cannot be placed, and it cannot be partially converted without splitting the PE into two variants, which is outside the scope of a rewrite onto the block. This is also why the remaining FF→multiply→FF path shape cannot be eliminated everywhere.

Every other candidate in the table is converted by this plan.

ASSUMES: (1) In `activation`, `activation_type` does not go 1→0→1 within one activation pass — the mode bit at `REG_ACTIVATION_CSR_ADDR` (8'h3A, written at 4913, reset value 1 at 4838) is programmed before a pass and held while `enable_activation` and `in_data_available | activation_in_progress` stay high (5466, 5477, 5509, 5517). That is the one trajectory where the original's ReLU-branch hold (5480-5489) carries a non-zero `slope_applied_data_internal`/`data_intercept_delayed` across the ReLU window and then publishes it at 5534, which a block that clears instead of holding cannot reproduce. (2) In `norm`, `inv_var` (4889, read at 5070) and `validity_mask_a_rows` (4893-4894, read as `validity_mask` at 5068/5071) are not changed between two consecutive edges that both load `mean_applied_data`/`variance_applied_data` (5059, 5067-5076) — i.e. they are programmed before `start_tpu` (4884-4885) and held while norm is active. Line 5070 samples `inv_var` and `validity_mask[i]` one edge later than the `mean_applied_data` operand it multiplies, and the block samples all of its operands at one edge; reproducing the original's later sample would require the next-cycle value of those cfg registers, which exists only by duplicating the APB write decode of `cfg` (4874-4913) inside `norm`. A change while norm is idle is harmless (the previous edge cleared the block, so `result` is 0, which is what the original's cleared `mean_applied_data` also yields).
The reference testbench does not know either assumption, but its stimulus does not reach either case: for (1) it would need two APB writes of `PADDR==8'h3A` with opposite `PWDATA[0]` inside one ~18-cycle activation pass (`cycle_count` limit `DESIGN_SIZE+2`, 5504) while its random phase redraws `PSEL`, `PENABLE`, `PWRITE` and all 8 `PADDR` bits independently; for (2) every APB write of 8'h08/8'h0a/0x20 happens before `start_tpu` is set (`config_blocks` then the 0x04 write), `wait_done` only reads, and the random phase never writes 0x04 (it redirects that address to 0x00), so `start_tpu` stays 0 and `matmul_c_data_available` — norm's `in_data_available` (5898) — stays low while `inv_var` and the mask are being changed. All other trajectories (constant mask/`inv_var` during a pass, any change while idle, constant or singly-toggling `activation_type`) are reproduced exactly.

DESIGN_CHANGE: Two source changes would remove both assumptions and unlock more of the design. (1) In `activation`, give the ReLU branch (5486) explicit assignments for the tanH datapath registers (clear them, or run the tanH pipeline unconditionally and select only at 5534), so the datapath has no `activation_type`-dependent hold; the 16 blocks would then need only `reset | ~enable_activation | ~(in_data_available | activation_in_progress)`. (2) In `norm`, register `inv_var` and `validity_mask` locally and use those for both 5069 and 5070 (or give the `* inv_var` its own pipeline stage), so both multiply operands come from the same clock edge. Separately, the PE multiply (`qmult`, 256 instances) could use the block only if the PE array were time-multiplexed or the device had ≥256 DSP tiles.

4667	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
4668	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
4669	input reset;
4670	input clk;
4671	output [`DWIDTH-1:0] out;
4672	
4673	reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] out_temp;
4674	wire [`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out;
4675	wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] add_out;
4676	
4677	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] a_flopped;
4678	reg [`DWIDTH-1:0] b_flopped;
4679	
4680	wire [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_temp;
4681	reg [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] mul_out_temp_reg;
4682	
4683	always @(posedge clk) begin
4684	    a_flopped <= a;
4685	    b_flopped <= b;
4686	end
4687	
4688	//assign mul_out = a * b;
4689	qmult mult_u1(.i_multiplicand(a_flopped), .i_multiplier(b_flopped), .o_result(mul_out_temp));
4690	
4691	always @(posedge clk) begin
4692	    mul_out_temp_reg <= mul_out_temp;
4693	end
4694	
4695	//we just truncate the higher bits of the product
4696	//assign add_out = mul_out + out;
4697	qadd add_u1(.a(out_temp), .b(mul_out_temp_reg), .c(add_out));
4698	
4699	always @(posedge clk) begin
4700	    if (reset)
4701	        out_temp <= 0;
4702	    else
4703	        out_temp <= add_out;
4704	end
4705	
4706	//down cast the result
4707	assign out = 
4708	    (out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] == 0) ?  //positive number
4709	        (
4710	           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 1, that means overlfow
4711	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b1}}} : //sign bit and then all 1s
4712	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} 
4713	        )
4714	        : //negative number
4715	        (
4716	           (|(out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-2 : `DWIDTH-1])) ?  //is any bit from 14:7 is 0, that means overlfow
4717	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , out_temp[`DWIDTH-2:0]} :
4718	             {out_temp[2*`DWIDTH-1] , {(`DWIDTH-1){1'b0}}} //sign bit and then all 0s
4719	        );
4720	
4721	endmodule
4722	
4723	module qmult(i_multiplicand,i_multiplier,o_result);
4724	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplicand;
4725	input [`DWIDTH-1:0] i_multiplier;
4726	output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] o_result;
4727	
4728	assign o_result = i_multiplicand * i_multiplier;
4729	//DW02_mult #(`DWIDTH,`DWIDTH) u_mult(.A(i_multiplicand), .B(i_multiplier), .TC(1'b1), .PRODUCT(o_result));
4730	
4731	endmodule

4734	input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] a;
4735	input [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] b;
4736	output [2*`DWIDTH-1:0] c;

1	        // tpu_like.small.os.fixed at its top: the ws drive sequence on the os register map and its stride-16
2	        // layout. Run 1 matmul alone; the APB write that pulses pe_reset (the design's own one-cycle pulse,
3	        // with the array quiet: the protocol); a full reset; run 2 every block; a full reset; run 3 matmul
4	        // and norm; the random phase (APB traffic on the register set except the start/done word, BRAM
5	        // writes, reset 1 in 1024). Under START_ONLY the full resets and the random resets are left out.
6	        // Under PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT (the diagnostic) one more APB write of the pe_reset bit lands while run
7	        // 2's multiply is in flight, which the protocol excludes.
8	        fill_matrices;
9	        phase = "run1";
10	        load_matrices(0, 10'd0, 10'd0);
11	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd0); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
12	        apb_write(8'h00, 32'h1); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
13	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
14	        phase = "readback1"; read_c(10'd768, "run 1 (matmul)");
15	        phase = "pe_reset"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // the pe_reset bit, array quiet
16	        zero_cycles(4);
17	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
18	        phase = "run2";
19	        load_matrices(1, 10'd256, 10'd256);
20	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd256); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
21	        config_blocks(32'hf); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
22	`ifdef PE_RESET_IN_FLIGHT
23	        zero_cycles(30); phase = "pe_reset-live"; apb_write(8'h04, 32'h8000);   // a one-cycle pe_reset with products in flight
24	`endif
25	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
26	        phase = "readback2"; read_c(10'd768, "run 2 (every block)");
27	        if (`MIDRUN_RESETS) begin phase = "reset-pulse"; full_reset; end
28	        phase = "run3";
29	        load_matrices(2, 10'd512, 10'd512);
30	        apb_write(8'h0e, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h12, 32'd512); apb_write(8'h16, 32'd768);
31	        config_blocks(32'h3); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h1);
32	        wait_done(400); apb_write(8'h04, 32'h0);
33	        phase = "readback3"; read_c(10'd768, "run 3 (matmul and norm, after reset)");
34	        phase = "random"; ph = 0;
35	        for (i = 0; i < `RANDOM_CYCLES; i = i + 1) begin
36	            tick; set_data;
37	            case (ph)
38	                0: begin PSEL = 1; draw; PWRITE = r32[0]; PADDR = reg_addr(r32[5:1]); if (PADDR == 8'h04) PADDR = 8'h00; PENABLE = 0; apb_addr = PADDR; apb_write_q = PWRITE; apb_wdata = PWDATA; end   // no random start or pe_reset write: the protocol
39	                1: begin PSEL = 1; PENABLE = 1; PADDR = apb_addr; PWRITE = apb_write_q; PWDATA = apb_wdata; end   // set_data redrew them; the access cycle keeps the setup cycle's (APB: stable through setup and access)
40	                2: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; PWRITE = 0; end
41	                default: begin PSEL = 0; PENABLE = 0; end
42	            endcase

Read completed /work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v
input
{
 "file_path": "/work/sim_reference/mult_add_int_18x19.v"
}
output
1	// mult_add_int_18x19: result = ax*ay + bx + chainin, unsigned, the product and bx zero-extended
2	// to 64 bits, modulo 2^64 (the sum wraps at the result width).
3	// Ports: clk, reset, mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19] in;
4	// result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19] out. mode_sigs and scanin are not read (the scan chain is
5	// unused and tied to 0 in every spec).
6	// Registers (the arch's annotation read literally, decision 2026-10-16): every input is registered
7	// at the block's entry at one clock edge (ax, ay, bx and chainin alike); the product and
8	// the sum are formed from those registers and loaded into result at the next edge.
9	// Cycles: ax, ay, bx and chainin to result 2. chainout is combinational from the input
10	// registers: the sum result loads at the next edge unless that edge clears it (1 cycle from the
11	// inputs).
12	// scanout is the ay register (ay to scanout 1), as before; nothing specifies it.
13	// Reset: synchronous, active high (2026-10-17: the arch's annotation does not distinguish a reset
14	// register from a clear sampled by the registers themselves): at a clock edge that samples it high
15	// every register of the block (operand registers, chainin register, result) is cleared to 0; the block
16	// loads again at the next edge that samples it low. No register has an initial value: every one is
17	// X until the first clear.
18	module mult_add_int_18x19 (
19	    input clk,
20	    input reset,
21	    input [11:0] mode_sigs,
22	    input [17:0] ax,
23	    input [18:0] ay,
24	    input [35:0] bx,
25	    input [63:0] chainin,
26	    input [18:0] scanin,
27	    output reg [63:0] result,
28	    output [63:0] chainout,
29	    output [18:0] scanout
30	);
31	
32	    // stage 1: the input registers
33	    reg [17:0] ax_reg;
34	    reg [18:0] ay_reg;
35	    reg [35:0] bx_reg;
36	    reg [63:0] chainin_reg;
37	
38	    // combinational middle: the product, the addend and chainin, from the registers
39	    wire [36:0] prod = ax_reg * ay_reg;                          // 18x19 -> 37, exact
40	    wire [63:0] sum  = prod + bx_reg + chainin_reg;              // zero-extended, modulo 2^64
41	
42	    always @(posedge clk) begin
43	        if (reset) begin
44	            result <= 0;
45	            ax_reg <= 0; ay_reg <= 0; bx_reg <= 0; chainin_reg <= 0;
46	        end
47	        else begin
48	            ax_reg <= ax; ay_reg <= ay; bx_reg <= bx; chainin_reg <= chainin;
49	            result <= sum;                                       // stage 2
50	        end
51	    end
52	
53	    assign chainout = sum;                                  // the sum of the input registers
54	    assign scanout  = ay_reg;
55	
56	endmodule

prompt
Task

You are the plan reviewer. You did not write this plan. Check it against the design source, the block's contract and the correctness rules; approve it if it violates none.

The block
  • primitive to instantiate: mult_add_int_18x19 (precision INT19)
  • computes: out = ax*ay + bx + chainin
  • input ports: clk[1] (clock), reset[1], mode_sigs[12], ax[18], ay[19], bx[36], chainin[64], scanin[19]
  • output ports: result[64], chainout[64], scanout[19]
  • arithmetic: the block reads each operand port as an unsigned number, and its result is unsigned. An operand narrower than its port is zero-extended to the port width. The result wraps at 64 bits: a value past the result width keeps its low 64 bits (modulo 2^64), with no saturation and no flag.
  • registers: on clk, the block registers its operands (ax, ay, bx) at one clock edge and result at the next, with the arithmetic between the two registers: operands applied before clock edge k appear in result after edge k+1, 2 cycles later. The block has no clock enable and no path around either register. chainin is registered at the block's entry like an operand and reaches result 2 cycles after it is applied. chainout is combinational from the input registers: it carries the value result loads at the next clock edge unless that edge clears it, one cycle before result shows it.
  • reset: reset is synchronous and active high: at a clock edge that samples it high it clears every register of the block (the operand registers, the chainin register and the result register) to 0, and the block loads again at the next edge that samples it low.
  • power-up: the block's registers have no initial value. Each loads its input at every clock edge where reset is low, so, for a block whose inputs, chainin included, are defined, the result is defined from the second edge after power-up, with or without a reset. A rewrite may not rely on the result before then.
  • tie-off: chainin = 0 when the block is not chained (a dedicated chain input: only another block's chainout or a constant may drive it); scanin = 0 always (the operand scan chain is not used: ay is driven directly, and scanout is left unconnected); mode_sigs = 0 always (the architecture gives the port no meaning for this mode).
  • connections: connect every input port, by the port names above. chainout reaches only the next block's chainin, through a dedicated connection to the block directly below it in the column; it has no connection to general routing, so nothing else may read it.
The block on this architecture

These are read from the architecture file.

element on a pathns
into the block: a path ends at the block's input register (setup)0.019
inside the block: input register clock-to-Q + delay + output register setup0.060 + 2.140 + 0.019 = 2.219
out of the block: a path starts at the output register of result (clock-to-Q)0.060
chainout: input register clock-to-Q + delay, to the output pin0.060 + 2.140 = 2.200
soft flip-flop, clock to output0.060
  • Blocks in series are separate stages. A path through chainout starts at an input register and continues, in the same stage, into what it drives.
  • A block of this mode takes a DSP tile alone.
  • The block has chain ports: a block's chainout can drive the next block's chainin directly, so a sum can continue from block to block.
  • The device has 132 DSP tiles.
  • One DSP tile's area is 253779 minimum-width transistor areas and one CLB's 27905.
The design

The design source is tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v. The candidate regions the planner was offered:

An id names a product by its module and its multiply. One that ends in (sum_in=<module>) is a product whose sum sits in that other module: the product leaves its own module through a port and is added there, so the arithmetic to convert spans both. A #1, #2 after an id separates instances of the same product that sit in sums of different shapes.

Correctness: these must hold
  • Same behavior. The rewritten design must produce the same outputs as the original on every cycle, with every register of the original starting at 0. Do not change what the design computes. Logic added only to reproduce it (recomputing a value, deriving the block's reset, correcting a block's result) is allowed.
  • Visibility. Only what the block's output ports carry is visible outside it. A value read by anything else (another expression, a module port, another module) cannot go inside a block: keep it outside, or recompute it. Check the source for every reader.
  • Terms. Keep every term of the original arithmetic. A term with no matching block port is computed outside the block. A port the design ties to a constant may be tied the same way on the block.
  • Registers and reset. The block's registers, if it has any, behave as the contract states. A design register may become a block register only if that keeps its behavior. Drive the block's reset so that "same behavior" holds after every reset, not only at start-up. Registers the rewrite adds start unknown; do not rely on their initial value.
  • Assumptions. If the rewrite is correct only under an assumption about the design's inputs or use (a bound on a value, when a signal is asserted), state it on a line starting ASSUMES: and cite the lines of the design that support it. The reference testbench does not know your assumption, so the rewrite must still match the original on the testbench's stimulus, which does not respect it.
  • Scope. Change only what the rewrite onto the block needs.
Check
  • Every claim the plan makes about the design (who reads a value, a register's reset or enable, widths, cited lines, ASSUMES lines) against the source.
  • That the mapping as described keeps the same behavior.
  • That the plan states everything the planner's output section requires, listed below.

Do not judge cost or style; measurement decides cost.

What the plan must state
  • Every candidate region, converted or left, with the reason each is left: a correctness reason, or "not chosen this pass"; candidates that share a reason may be grouped.
  • How many blocks the rewrite adds.
  • For each converted region, the register stages from its operands to its result, before and after the rewrite.
Output
  • First line exactly APPROVE or REVISE.
  • If REVISE, for each problem: the rule it violates or the required item it omits, by name; the design lines; what to change.
  • Write nothing before the verdict line; read the source first. The verdict is applied automatically, and your reply is read by the framework, not answered by a person, so do not end with a question or an offer.
What is currently built and measured

The kept candidate is first pass. tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v in this directory is the unmodified source as given to you, which the plan under review rewrites afresh; the kept candidate is the plan below, applied to that same source, and the diff below is that edit.

The kept candidate's plan

TIER: T2
REGIONS: activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$470, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$474, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$478, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$482, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$486, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$490, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$494, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$498, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$502, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$506, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$510, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$514, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$518, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$522, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$526, activation.$mul$tpu_like.small.os.fixed.v:5482$530
    ```
